afternoon near Warwick Castle. It omitted the wasp-waist costume, the missing manuscript, and the instruction to help establish a republic. Cassian read that last item twice. \"I married a woman of unconventional ambition.\"","\"Really? I thought you married me for my diplomacy.\"","A funny noise escaped June, who had accompanied them to cover the literary festival. She looked up from her blog and declined to comment. June wanted to emphasize the family contributors in her account.","The Paper Republic was a hybrid of writing workshop and theatrical game. Visitors of all stripes could draft a constitution, dispute a ban, or appeal a fictional judgment. The instructor used common law as a comparison, emphasizing how earlier decisions could guide later ones. A tropical pavilion supplied iced tea. Beyond it, a classical quartet competed with an aerobic dance class. The mixture made everyone laugh; the little republic's daily bulletin described the noise as public discourse.","Their first chat took place over a bowl of nuts, which Cassian quietly moved away from the fragile manuscript. Elspeth's accent sharpened whenever the publisher approached. Mara noticed the change without treating it as an indication of dishonesty. It suggested discomfort, and the catalogue's ambiguity gave her reason to ask why.","Mara met its founder, Elspeth Row, beside a printer that refused to rotate. The elderly writer's biography called her a feminine icon; her autobiography preferred the story of a girl who once sold repaired shoes. \"They edited out the feet,\" she said. \"Very elegant. Also false.\"","A formative chapter concerned her mother's diary. Its local dialect, preserved in dictation by a granddaughter, had become the heart of a novel. The publisher proposed a mass-market franchise around it. Elspeth wanted to publish the diary itself, with guidance from its inheritors. Her insistence on that point had caused a sizable controversy. One reviewer called the language ignorant; another treated every idiom as quaint. Neither, she said, had listened to the actual voice.","June asked whether she could subscribe to the new journal Elspeth was funding. \"Of course. It contains an epic argument about copyright, followed by a recipe. We believe in brevity, except when hungry.\"","At the auction desk, bidding had begun for an early draft. A citation linked it to the diary, and the catalogue placed a torn note in the same context as a letter granting permission. Mara inspected the paper. The note held a cipher: numbers matching words in an old encyclopedia. Its meaning was the donor's supposed affirmation of the sale. Yet the handwriting belonged to the writer's aunt, not her mother.","The auctioneer produced an e-mail with the subject line PERMISSION CONFIRMED. Mara was cautious of an assurance that required ignoring the missing signature. The accompanying certificate could certify the paper's age, not the author's intent. She asked him to re-evaluate the entry before the hammer fell. The request brought an immediate howl from a man who had already promised the manuscript to his client.","\"Average people don't understand these distinctions,\" he said.","\"Then explain them without disrespect,\" Elspeth answered.","The man was the franchise director, Perrin Dale. His boast that he had \"saved\" Elspeth's career made her stare. He treated the auction as a commonplace commercial affair, calling copyright an obstacle to creativity. June observed the hypocrisy of praising an individual voice while ignoring its owner. Perrin replied that he would forbid anyone to disrupt the sale.","Mara caught Cassian's eye. Their eye contact was enough. He moved to the desk, while she asked Elspeth to show her the diary. It was gone from its locked glass case.","\"Hooray,\" Cassian murmured. \"Our restful afternoon has arrived.\"","The tone rescued Elspeth from panic. She ordered staff to huddle by the entrance and check who had carried material through. They would not preach honesty at strangers while making an untested accusation. Her hypothesis was that someone had taken the diary to support the sale, but an indication was not proof. She asked the festival manager to set out the access records.","Mara examined the empty mount. A tiny scrap of artwork, printed with the workshop's republic seal, clung to its hinge. The same design appeared on the souvenir diploma participants could customize. Someone had carried the case's key in a folded certificate. Mara's pleasure at finding it was spoiled by the realization that innumerable visitors owned one.","June checked the publisher's digest. The annals of the old shoe factory appeared as a triumphant account of prosperity, omitting the years of low wages. A table used the consumer price index to compare wages over time, but the reproduced figures cut off one column. That might be careless editing. It also made poverty appear less severe. The context could change the entire story.","Perrin's assistant called the missing diary a trivial distraction from the successful launch. Elspeth's face hardened. \"You put my name on an exaggeration. Now my mother's words disappear.\"","Mara found a hint in the assistant's bag: two identical copies of the permission page, one with a line inserted. The dialog printed beneath Elspeth's signature had been altered to suggest approval. A falsehood could look respectable beneath a familiar name. June photographed the pages with permission, taking care to preserve their order.","The festival's little writing bible said never to confuse a narrator with an author. Perrin had ignored its most useful rule. His dogma was that a dominant brand could speak for everyone. The stolen notebook might expose the original exchange. Cassian asked where the publisher kept his exhibit material.","\"A storage room behind the stage,\" Elspeth said. \"The door sticks.\"","\"A complement to my other qualifications,\" he answered. \"I'm excellent with stubborn doors.\"","Mara took his arm. They walked past a child who could only gape at their costumes. Cassian bowed, Elspeth laughed, and the pursuit began. The gradual recovery of her anger had done more than any promise to calm her.","\"We'll party with the republic later,\" Mara told June. \"First we get its mother's diary back.\"","June closed her notebook. \"That will contribute to a much better story.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1014,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0166\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The A visitor's constitution lay beneath a law student's model appeal. A mock ban on dancing had already been reversed, with a comparison to common law scribbled in the margin. Cassian stepped over a fallen classical column. \"The republic has suffered structural difficulties.\" A bowl of nuts lay spilled beneath a chair.","\"Nothing my diplomacy can't repair,\" Mara said, lifting its paper roof.","June entered behind them, her e-mail inbox filling with demands for an exclusive. The subject line of one message read DUCHESS SEIZES FREE PRESS. \"Someone has an epic gift for exaggeration.\"","The diary stood open on Perrin's desk. Its local dialect was not obscure to anyone willing to read slowly. Mara recognized the missing sentence immediately. Elspeth's mother had refused a proposal to sell private family letters. The permission Perrin advertised concerned a different article. His catalogue had placed one document in the same context as another, making the distinction practically invisible.","Elspeth confirmed that the cipher followed her mother's recorded dictation. Her accent carried the same rhythm. A short dialog with June clarified the disputed phrase, and she gave an explicit affirmation that the heirs had not agreed to the sale. Perrin's insistence that a signature settled copyright was a convenient dogma, not a reason to ignore the page above it. The chat lasted less than a minute.","A numbered cipher appeared beside the refusal. June brought up the scanned encyclopedia on her phone. With guidance from the unaltered key, it yielded an old family idiom: \"Keep the roof, share the fire.\" In the diary's context, the mother offered public stories while retaining her private home. Perrin had translated that into unconditional permission. The resulting ambiguity had enriched his client.","\"Not merely careless editing,\" June said. \"An active falsehood.\"","Perrin arrived with two attendants. He ordered them to forbid photographs, then announced a ban on unauthorized access. Elspeth followed him, walking more quickly than her age suggested. \"I authorized it,\" she said. \"And the diary's owners asked them to find it.\"","The director called her ungrateful. His formative contribution to her career, he said, entitled him to shape the franchise. His boast made June's pen pause. She asked whether he wanted that statement recorded. He insisted he did. Elspeth gave a short, funny laugh with no pleasure in it.","Mara wanted him to re-evaluate his position before she showed the copied page. Her pleasure at doing so was admittedly considerable. But she was cautious of turning a correction into a performance that swallowed the writer's voice. She set out the documents on the desk and let Elspeth read the difference herself.","Outside, the aerobic class began its tropical dance. An instructor shouted \"Hooray!\" so loudly that everyone looked toward the window. Perrin seized the moment to snatch the notebook and run. Cassian moved the table out of his path before Mara could climb over it in her wasp-waist costume. She thanked him with a glance and followed through the narrow door.","The attendants formed a huddle near the printer. One man described Perrin's disrespect toward a volunteer who would not customize the display to hide the refusal. That was a useful indication of what had happened, though the volunteer herself would need to speak. The director's hybrid excuse combined urgency with flattery, and innumerable small demands had worn her down.","The director's route crossed a workshop where people of all stripes were composing a daily bulletin. A girl looked up from her diploma design. A man customizing the republic's seal caught the falling ink tray as Perrin struck the table. Its sizable stain spread across the director's elegant sleeve. He gave a howl and nearly dropped the diary.","\"Don't rotate it,\" Mara warned when he grabbed the glass display stand for balance. \"The base is loose.\"","He did. The stand turned, the polished portrait tilted, and Cassian caught its frame before it hit the girl. Mara took the diary from Perrin's suddenly occupied hand. Its binding was undamaged. The child, more impressed by the recovery than the danger, began to gape. June checked that she was unhurt before speaking to the attendants.","Security asked for a brief account. Elspeth's insistence on brevity survived the moment: \"He took our diary. Those two retrieved it. I would like it returned to its owners.\"","No one needed to preach. The law student offered to help the manager preserve the access record. Her fiction about common law had given way to a genuine question about responsibility. She would not certify facts she had not seen. The attendants, previously ignorant of the dispute, gave their own accounts instead of repeating Perrin's.","Back at the auction, bidding stopped. The manager posted a correction on the blog and in the bulletin. Its first draft was a digest so compressed that it obscured the owners' refusal. June asked him to emphasize the actual distinction. She would not subscribe to a version that made Elspeth seem unable to understand her own past.","The biography in the catalogue had treated her as an icon of feminine endurance. Her autobiography was less convenient. It described anger, failed jobs, and a mother whose diary would never be a corporate bible. The chapter Perrin called commonplace had supplied his most profitable artwork. That contradiction was the hypocrisy the public needed to understand.","Mara checked the reproduced wage table. An accountant from the audience explained the missing consumer price index column and supplied the original citation. The factory's annals had not supported the publisher's rosy account. The discovery made them question his broader hypothesis about prosperity, while leaving room for other records. A scholarly discourse could be vigorous without making certainty its dominant style.","An assistant offered a mass-market compromise: sell selected pages and call the controversy solved. Elspeth declined. A gradual agreement might emerge after the heirs discussed it. The sale was not a complement to their wishes merely because it paid them. She made eye contact with the manager and asked for that to appear in tomorrow's bulletin.","Mara's costume seam gave a warning snap. Cassian put his coat around her before anyone else noticed. \"Unconventional editing,\" he whispered.","\"Really.\"","\"A hint that it's time to party with your husband.\"","She laughed. Beyond the stage, a sign pointed toward Warwick Castle. Their excursion had acquired a better ending than its brochure promised."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1068,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0167\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Republic","anchor":"chapter-0168","paragraphs":["The little republic reopened its parliament at dusk. A child proposed a ban on adults interrupting. An adult submitted an appeal, citing common law with more confidence than accuracy. The law student returned both proposals with guidance and a suggestion that the constitution permit everyone to finish a sentence. It was an unconventional lesson in diplomacy, and Mara enjoyed every minute. The child took that as an indication of success.","Cassian sat beside her with his coat still around her shoulders. The wasp-waist costume had surrendered its dominant position to comfort. \"I like this chapter,\" he said.","\"The one where nothing is stolen?\"","\"The one where you remain within kissing distance.\"","The festival's daily bulletin now carried the corrected auction notice. Bidding would resume only for materials whose owners had agreed to sell them. The permission page was withdrawn. A clear citation linked the account to the actual documents, while a short explanation restored the consumer price index column to the wage table. People who wanted a digest could read the summary; those who wanted context could inspect the fuller version.","June posted her story on the blog. She avoided an epic account of her own cleverness. The formative event had been Elspeth's decision to challenge the publisher, and the diary's heirs had chosen what could be shown. Their privacy was not a quaint idiom waiting for an outsider to decode. The cipher remained an interesting object, explained with the help of the old encyclopedia and its original key.","A little controversy followed the first paragraph. One reader treated the biography as authoritative and dismissed the autobiography as self-serving. Another took the opposite view. June answered that each account had an author and a purpose. The presence of an icon on the cover could not settle a factual disagreement. She did not preach infallibility, nor subscribe to the dogma that two competing accounts must be equally persuasive.","Elspeth had eventually abandoned the franchise and its feminine marketing style. Her new cover showed an ordinary shoe shop, a commonplace scene she loved. The change was no exaggeration of her independence; it was simply a choice she could make. Staff formed a last huddle to settle which display would stay open.","Elspeth joined the workshop's dictation exercise. Her accent sounded stronger when she was amused. She read a passage in her mother's dialect and asked the participants to preserve its rhythm. It was a funny story about a pair of stolen boots. Several people laughed before a girl asked what one unfamiliar word meant. Elspeth explained it without making the child feel ignorant. The brief chat held more warmth than the franchise's polished promotional film.","Mara watched an apprentice draw the boots. The artwork was a complement to the words, not a claim to replace them. He asked Elspeth's permission before attaching the quotation. The festival's copyright discussion had become something he could practice. The little writing bible in his bag no longer seemed a collection of abstract rules.","Someone asked whether the novelist would now ban every adaptation. \"No,\" she said. \"One editor's disrespect doesn't require everyone else's punishment.\"","Her affirmation was followed by a sizable sigh of relief from the apprentice. She asked to see his hybrid comic version when it was ready. His gradual mastery of the work would depend on editing, and she would happily make suggestions. The prospect left him too astonished to do anything but gape.","At the dinner table, an auctioneer offered to buy everybody's drinks. The offer produced an immediate \"Hooray!\" and a good-natured howl from the café owner. She explained that the republic's constitution had no power over her till. The auctioneer paid, discovering that public discourse could have concrete financial consequences. Cassian laughed, his hand warm around Mara's.","The corrected sign cost roughly the price of two dinners. Perrin's lawyer threatened a nuisance-lawsuit tactic, but the manager kept the documents and asked his own adviser to reply. Mara left that work to them. She had a dance to attend.","The festival director brought Elspeth a corrected diploma. Its wording acknowledged the family contributors. The old one had made the publisher's boast of discovery look like an institutional judgment. Elspeth asked for no extravagant apology. Brevity, she said, was a kindness when the essential falsehood had been removed. The director promised the same correction in his e-mail announcement.","June checked the subject line. It said DIARY CLARIFICATION, which was accurate if joyless. \"Really,\" Elspeth said. \"We can do better.\"","After a short dialog, they agreed on THE WORDS BELONG TO THEIR WRITERS. It was not a legal ruling, but it gave the right hint about the evening. The annals of the Paper Republic would remember a messy day without converting it into an uncomplicated victory.","Elspeth looked toward the pavilion. \"Will the uncertainty hurt the book?\"","Mara understood the anxiety beneath the question. A dominant public story was easier to sell than an honest ambiguity. But putting a family's private correspondence in the same context as a commercial license would make the next dispute worse. She said that carefully, without claiming to know the future.","The writer nodded. \"A hypothesis worth testing.\"","June raised her glass to the phrase. People of all stripes clinked cups, including the accountant whose table had saved the wage comparison. The celebration lacked an official icon, a central doctrine, and any need to preach. It had food, music, and sufficient chairs.","Cassian leaned close. \"Now may I party with my wife?\"","They made eye contact, and Mara kissed him before he could improve the question. The quartet began a classical tune that the dancers cheerfully turned into something faster. The director's insistence on a formal farewell disappeared. Public hypocrisy, June declared, was best resisted by refusing to pretend nobody wanted dessert.","Mara laughed and let Cassian rotate her once beneath the lights. She had come for a quiet afternoon and acquired another memento of a life she could never have planned. This one smelled of ink and summer rain."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1000,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0168\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Paper Names","anchor":"chapter-0169","paragraphs":["The Night of Names began with a pink slip. Mara found it tucked beneath the lyric sheet for a jazz duet, where a petition should have been. The singer, Anja Roe, stared at the paper and said she had worked at the hall for twelve years. Cassian put down the drink he had bought for his wife. Their evening's musical ambitions could wait. The sanction felt brutally personal.","\"It says my passport is invalid,\" Anja explained. \"The organizer says I can't perform or work here.\"","The hall could cancel a booking; it could not decide her nationality. A grand pronouncement had turned a local contract dispute into a threat. June, who was writing for a cultural periodical, asked the organizer to weigh in directly. He sent a memo instead. Its rhetoric blamed new legislation, but the attached memorandum cited only a private software policy.","Mara was skeptical. A patent notice adorned the booking portal, advertising a voice-recognition system supplied by Oceanic Arts. The program assigned every performer a nickname and a nominal access fee. It was supposed to preempt duplicate entries. The programmer's pronunciation guide was evidently backward: it treated Anja's given name as a plural noun, then rejected the passport because the names differed.","Quell's first intimation of anger had been a cold little smile. The massive sponsor board could be compared to a family tree, with his name everywhere. Anja had been an obedient employee, but not an obedient artist. Her new poetry was the first thing he could not buy. Cassian's expression hardened. 'You don't get to ill-treat a performer because she stops flattering you,' he said.","\"A computer has fired me over grammar,\" Anja said.","\"A person chose to let it,\" Cassian answered.","The organizer, Varo Quell, stood beside an offline kiosk running Internet Explorer as part of an old-computing exhibit. Its pop-up displayed a joke quiz in Latin, and a child was reciting answers with absurd conviction. The obsolete machine did not control the booking system. Yet Quell pointed at it as if the whole scenario were an unavoidable accident of technology. The weather outside was changeable, and rain hammered the glass.","Mara asked him to affirm what he had actually done. He would not. Instead, he called her conventional, unqualified, and uncool. She smiled so sweetly that Cassian became immediately wary. Her retort was quiet: \"You sent a pink slip. Take responsibility for the ink.\"","The poet was keen to make his recitation memorable. His suburban writing group had met here to discuss literature for years, and he resented its rooms becoming an advertisement. A novel revenge occurred to him: make Quell pronounce every piece of jargon in the cancellation letter. Mara rejected it, laughing. The paradox was that mocking unclear language could make the actual error harder to see.","At the bar, a poet held a shabby journal full of revised verse. The preface thanked Anja for her years of help. She had enabled him to rehearse here for free while he worked a suburban day job. Now he wanted an intensive campaign, a resolution that would forcefully overturn Quell's decision. June asked him to preserve the evidence before making an oath to wreck the evening. His enthusiasm was plentiful; his facts were still incomplete.","The hall's original charter promised \"free social intercourse among artists.\" Anja's grandfather had written that phrase. Its old-fashioned prose belonged to a serious effort to make strangers welcome. Quell had used the same charter as a pretext to insist that nobody could question management during the show. His interpretation converted an invitation into a prohibition.","The poet's new poem made a metaphor of a locked door. Mara liked the line, though she asked him to cut out a reference to Quell's family. Satire did not require a rotten account of people who had done nothing. He accepted the correction, then crossed out a pronoun whose antecedent was unclear. In the margin, a note explained how one preposition had changed the meaning of the petition. The portal kept two exports for redundancy; one might preserve the original before the rumor's propagation.","The document opposed a specific fee. Quell's version turned it into opposition to all outside performers. That misinterpretation had already spread as misinformation. A party-line message told sponsors that hostile locals wanted foreign artists removed. Anja, who had helped invite those artists, was being blamed for words she had not signed. Her name preceded the others only because the form sorted names alphabetically.","Cassian watched her pale face. \"We'll find the original.\"","Quell announced that their query was delaying the show. He wanted to reap applause for defending diversity without answering the singer. A giant portraiture banner behind him showed Anja smiling in last year's costume. Its masculine tailoring had been his idea; he now claimed her appearance proved she had always liked his policy. The persuasion was so clumsy that several musicians looked away.","Mara turned from the banner to the postmark on Anja's notice. The postal service had delivered it yesterday, before the portal supposedly detected the error. The contradiction was of note. This might be more than a programming mistake. The riddle was why Quell had decided to remove her before the system gave him his excuse.","A drummer began a rap in the next room, its proverb about greed drawing a burst of laughter. The performance ended with a salute to the old hall. Quell bowed as if the tribute were his. June recorded the moment without adding a refutation of every boast. The irony spoke loudly enough.","They found the original petition in Anja's sent mail, along with the numerical booking record. A lyric in her forthcoming song described the \"toll on a borrowed voice.\" Quell had demanded that she delete it, claiming it endangered the patent owner. Anja refused. Subsequently, her booking had vanished. Her plea had been met with a lecture about the status quo.","Cassian asked whether she still wanted to sing. She hesitated, then nodded. \"If it's my choice.\"","Mara took her hand. \"Then let's make sure you have one.\"","Quell tried to block the stage door. Mara looked up at the fire-exit sign, then back at him. His face changed. He moved. The evening had acquired an imaginative twist he had not planned, and his own propaganda was about to meet its subject."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1055,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0169\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Night of Names</div><h1>The Name before the Door</h1><p id=\"chapter-0169-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5511\" data-words=\"5511\">Night</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5409\" data-words=\"5409\">Names</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-896\" data-words=\"896\">began</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9260\" data-words=\"9260\">with</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6179\" data-words=\"6179\">pink slip</a>.
